\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
\vskip-28.45274pt\end{center}
\par In addition, we also need to ensure that the elimination of the concatenations -- which leads to a non-deterministic change in the initial and final states of the automata -- does not disrupt the language accepted.
For this we will use the $!$ character, which marks the beginning and end of the value of $x_{1}$.
The automaton for $L_{V}$ will treat $!$ as a kind of ``reset'' character, which takes the automaton back to a defined initial state, which will also be the accepting state.
\par Before giving the formal definition, we will give an extract of the automaton that will check for the sequence
$b_{1}\ldots b_{n}$
and check the tiling relation.
Each state is of the form $\left(q,t\right)$ where $t$ is the previously saved tile.
In the diagram, $\$_{i}$ denotes the set of all characters
$\$^{b^{\prime}}_{i,b}$
for all $b,b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}$.
I.e.\ the transition can read any of these characters.
The top row of the automaton shows the run whilst the correct sequence is being read (and the tile needs to be checked), whilst the bottom is for all bit sequences that diverge from
$b_{1}\ldots b_{n}$
(another column is being read).
From $\left(q_{n},t\right)$ the tiles
$t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}$
are all tiles such that
$\left(t,t_{j}\right)\in V$
for all $j$.
The transitions from each
$\left(q_{1},t_{j}\right)$
will be analogous to those from
$\left(q_{1},t\right)$.
\par\par\par To see the pattern of the automaton extract, we show how it would be instantiated for $L_{0\ldots 00}$ with all edges labelled by
$\$^{b^{\prime}}_{i,b}$
removed.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
\end{center}
\par Hence, we are ready to define an automaton $\mathcal{A}_{V}$ giving the language $L_{V}$.
We give the definition first, and explanation below.
We define
$$\mathcal{A}_{V}=\left(Q,\delta,q_{0},F\right)$$
where
$$\begin{array}[]{rcl}Q&=&\{q_{0},q_{1},\ldots,q_{n},q_{n+1},p_{2},\ldots,p_{n},p_{n+1}\}\ \cup\\
&&\left\{{\left(q_{i},t\right)}\ \middle|\ {1\leq i\leq n+1\land t\in\Theta}\right\}\ \cup\\
&&\left\{{\left(p_{i},t\right)}\ \middle|\ {2\leq i\leq n+1\land t\in\Theta}\right\}\end{array}$$
$$\begin{array}[]{rcl}\delta&=&\left\{{q\xrightarrow{!}q_{0}}\ \middle|\ {q\in Q}\right\}\ \cup\\
&&\{q_{0}\xrightarrow{\#}q_{1}\}\ \cup\\
&&\left\{{\left(q_{1},t\right)\xrightarrow{\#}\left(q_{1},t\right)}\ \middle|\ {t\in\Theta}\right\}\ \cup\\
&&\left\{{q_{i}\xrightarrow{\$^{b}_{i,b}}q_{i+1}}\ \middle|\ {1\leq i\leq n\land b\in\{0,1\}}\right\}\ \cup\\
&&\left\{{\left(q_{i},t\right)\xrightarrow{\$^{b}_{i,b}}\left(q_{i+1},t\right)}\ \middle|\ {1\leq i\leq n\land b\in\{0,1\}}\right\}\ \cup\end{array}$$
$$\begin{array}[]{r c l}&&\left\{{q_{i}\xrightarrow{\$^{b^{\prime}}_{i,b}}p_{i+1}}\ \middle|\ {1\leq i\leq n\land b\neq b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}}\right\}\ \cup\\
&&\left\{{\left(q_{i},t\right)\xrightarrow{\$^{b^{\prime}}_{i,b}}\left(p_{i+1},t\right)}\ \middle|\ {1\leq i\leq n\land b\neq b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}}\right\}\ \cup\\
&&\left\{{p_{i}\xrightarrow{\$^{b^{\prime}}_{i,b}}p_{i+1}}\ \middle|\ {2\leq i\leq n\land b,b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}}\right\}\ \cup\\
&&\left\{{\left(p_{i},t\right)\xrightarrow{\$^{b^{\prime}}_{i,b}}\left(p_{i+1},t\right)}\ \middle|\ {2\leq i\leq n\land b,b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}}\right\}\ \cup\\
&&\left\{{q_{n+1}\xrightarrow{t}\left(q_{1},t\right)}\ \middle|\ {t\in\Theta}\right\}\ \cup\\
&&\left\{{\left(q_{n+1},t\right)\xrightarrow{t^{\prime}}\left(q_{1},t^{\prime}\right)}\ \middle|\ {\left(t,t^{\prime}\right)\in V}\right\}\ \cup\\
&&\left\{{\left(p_{n+1},t\right)\xrightarrow{t^{\prime}}\left(q_{1},t\right)}\ \middle|\ {t,t^{\prime}\in\Theta}\right\}\\
\\
F&=&\{q_{0}\}\end{array}$$
The first set of transitions in $\delta$ are the reset transitions.
From all states (including $q_{0}$) a $!$ will bring the automaton back to its initial state.
Since we enforce separately that the value of $x_{0}$ begins and ends with $!$, it will remain true through the concatenations and $\mathsf{replaceAll}$ operations that the value of $x_{i}$ still begins and ends with $!$.
Thus, the only final state that can occur on a solution to the string constraints is $q_{0}$ and the initial state is immaterial.
\par Next, we define the transitions over $\#$ which simply track the beginning of a new row.
Since we enforce separately that the input is of the correct format, we can simply use a self-loop on $\left(q_{1},t\right)$ to pass over $\#$ -- it will only be used once.
\par The next transitions read
$\$^{b}_{i,b}$
characters.
This means that in
$L_{b_{1}\ldots b_{n}}$
we are reading the value $b=b_{i}$ at position $i$.
Thus, this is part of the sequence of bits identifying the column
$L_{b_{1}\ldots b_{n}}$
is checking.
Thus, we continue reading the sequence of bits in the $q$ states.
\par Conversely, we next deal with the case when
$\$^{b^{\prime}}_{i,b}$
with $b\neq b^{\prime}$ is being read.
That is, we are reading a column that is not the
$b_{1}\ldots b_{n}$th
and hence we simply skip over it by using the $p$ states.
\par Finally, we read the tiles.
If we are in state $q_{n}$ then we are reading the
$b_{1}\ldots b_{n}$th
column, but this is the first row (after a reset) and thus there is no matching relation to verify.
If we are in state $\left(q_{n+1},t\right)$ then we are reading the
$b_{1}\ldots b_{n}$th
column, but this is not the first row and we have to verify the matching relation.
Wherefrom, there is only a transition reading $t^{\prime}$ if
$\left(t,t^{\prime}\right)\in V$.
The tile $t^{\prime}$ is then saved for comparison with the next tile.
\par Otherwise, if we are in a state $p_{n+1}$ or $\left(p_{n+1},t\right)$ then we are not in the correct column and there is nothing to verify.
In this case we simply skip over the tile and continue.
\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Completing the Proof}
\par Given a tiling problem over a corridor of width $2^{n}$, we have given a (polynomially sized in $n$)
$\mathsf{SL}[\mathsf{replaceAll}]$
constraint $\varphi$ using languages
$L$, $L_{V}$, $L_{H}$, $L_{1}$, \ldots, $L_{n}$
(representable by automata polynomially sized in $n$)
such that $\varphi$ is satisfiable iff the variable $x_{1}$ takes on a value
$$!\#{0}_{1}\ldots{0}_{n}t^{1}_{1}{0}_{1}\ldots{1}_{n}t^{1}_{2}\ldots{1}_{1}\ldots{1}_{n}t^{1}_{\ell}\#\ldots\#{0}_{1}\ldots{0}_{n}t^{h}_{1}\ldots{1}_{1}\ldots{1}_{n}t^{h}_{\ell}\#!$$
such that
$$\#t^{1}_{1}\ldots t^{1}_{\ell}\#t^{2}_{1}\ldots t^{2}_{\ell}\#\ldots\#t^{h}_{1}\ldots t^{h}_{\ell}\#$$
is a solution to the tiling problem.
Since such a tiling problem is $2^{n}$-SPACE-hard, we have shown {expspace}-hardness of
the satisfiability problem for $\mathsf{SL}[\mathsf{replaceAll}]$. Moreover, this holds even in the single-letter case.
\par\par\par
\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:length}}
\par\@proof The proof follows the same line as that of Theorem~\ref{thm-ftconrev}. The decision procedure consists of four steps, where the first two steps are the same as those in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm-ftconrev}.
\par The third step is adapted by applying the following additional transformation to length assertions: When splitting each variable $x$ into multiple fresh variables, say, $x_{1}\ldots x_{k}$, replace all occurrences of $|x|$ (resp. $|x|_{a}$) in length assertions by $\sum\limits_{1\leq i\leq k}|x_{i}|$ (resp. $\sum\limits_{1\leq i\leq k}|x_{i}|_{a}$).
\par The fourth step is adapted by applying the following additional transformation to length assertions: For each variable $x\in X$, if $x^{(r)}$ occurs in the assignments of $S_{4}$, then replace each occurrence of $|x|$ (resp. $|x|_{a}$) with $|x^{(r)}|$ (resp. $|x^{(r)}|_{a}$), otherwise, replace each occurrence of $|x^{(r)}|$ (resp. $|x^{(r)}|_{a}$) with $|x|$ (resp. $|x|_{a}$).
\par It follows that we obtain a symbolic execution of polynomial size that contains only FT{}s, regular constraints and length assertions. The {expspace}{} upper bound follows from \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{LB16}{}{}, Theorem~12]}.
\qed\end@proof
